        // Init service worker
        if ('serviceWorker' in navigator) {

    <iframe id='rpiframe' name='rpiframe' src="https://assetstore.unity.com/public/pages/auth/rpiframe.html#client_id=asset_store_v2&amp;session_state=" width="0" height="0" border="0" style="width: 0;height: 0; border: none; display: none;"></iframe>
    <iframe id='opiframe' name='opiframe' src="https://api.unity.com/static/oauth2/opiframe.html" width="0" height="0" border="0" style="width: 0; height: 0; border: none; display: none;"></iframe>
    

    <script nonce="VUwUwrhwT1z2rVgT" type="text/javascript">
        var disableAutoLogin = false;
        function getCookie(a){a+="=";for(var d=document.cookie.split(";"),b=0;b<d.length;b++){var c=d[b].trim();if(0==c.indexOf(a))return c.substring(a.length,c.length)}return""};
        function receiveMessage(e) {
            if (disableAutoLogin) {
                return;
            }
            switch (e.data) {
              case "sessionLoggedIn":
                window.location = "/auth/login?redirect_to=%2Fpackages%2Ftools%2Fvisual-scripting%2Fdotween-pro-32416";
                break;
              case "sessionLoggedOut":
                // STABILITY: sessionLoggedOut handler disabled. The rpiframe/opiframe
                // OIDC session-monitoring iframes were force-logging users out within
                // ~3 seconds of arriving on any ASv2 page, because ASv2's stored
                // session_state (captured once at login in buildLoginSession) drifts
                // from what the central auth service currently reports — especially
                // after the recent central-auth security update.
                //
                // Users now keep their session until a real Genesis-backed call
                // returns 401, at which point GraphQLProvider.js redirects through
                // /auth/login for a transparent OAuth round-trip.
                break;
              case "disableAutoLogin":
                disableAutoLogin = true;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (window.addEventListener) {
            window.addEventListener('message', receiveMessage);
        } else {
            window.attachEvent('onmessage', receiveMessage);
        }
    </script>
    <!-- End of Login/Logout Passively -->

complete modular building framework for Unity. Everything you need to implement a full building system in any project. Extensible, source-included, and built to scale.","keyFeatures":"<p ><strong>Building Manager</strong></p><p >One Building Manager per scene, and everything registers to it.</p><p >Building Parts, Sockets, Areas and Groups sign in when they turn on.</p><p >Look them up by type, by ID, or by distance.</p><p >It also owns the sub-systems: Grid, Grouping, Batching, Save, Terrain and Physics.</p><p >Turn on only the ones your game needs.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Controller</strong></p><p >Goes on your player or your camera.</p><p >Holds the current build mode, the active view and the selected Building Part.</p><p >Sends the player input to whichever Building State is active.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Input</strong></p><p >Reads validate, cancel, rotate and select, then hands them to the active state.</p><p >Works with the legacy Input Manager and the new Input System, out of the box.</p><p >Keyboard, mouse, gamepad and touch are all covered.</p><p >Building input stops while the cursor is over your UI.</p></br><p ><strong>Building States</strong></p><p >One state per build mode, each with its own Enter, Update and Exit.</p><p >Placement previews the part, snaps to sockets, rotates and aligns to the grid.</p><p >Destruction removes a placed part, with its own feedback.</p><p >Adjustment moves a placed part while keeping its connections.</p><p >Upgrade switches a placed part to another variant, in place.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingState to add your own mode.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Views</strong></p><p >A view answers one question: where does the player aim right now?</p><p >First Person, Third Person, Top Down and Orbital are included.</p><p >Each one builds its own build ray, and you can swap views while the game runs.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingView to support your own camera.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Part</strong></p><p >The placeable object, set up entirely in the Inspector.</p><p >Preview visuals, placement, conditions and behaviors all live on one component.</p><p >Presets let several parts share the same setup.</p><p >Renderer variants cover visual styles and upgrade tiers.</p><p >An editor tool turns any model into a ready-to-use prefab, in one click.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Conditions</strong></p><p >A condition decides whether an action is allowed, and says why when it is not.</p><p >They check socket attachment, areas, collisions, ground contact, support,</p><p >terrain layers, nearby trees and slope limits.</p><p >Conditions run in order, and the first failure blocks the action.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingCondition to write your own.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Behaviors</strong></p><p >A behavior reacts once the action has already happened, so it never blocks.</p><p >Play an animation on placement, destruction, adjustment or upgrade.</p><p >Spawn physics debris when a part is destroyed or collapses.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingBehavior for your own effects.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Socket</strong></p><p >A snap point where Building Parts attach to each other.</p><p >One socket holds several snapping points, each with its own offset.</p><p >Filter what may connect, by Building Part or by category.</p><p >Priorities decide which socket wins when two of them are close.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Grid System</strong></p><p >Snaps previews to fixed cells, and tracks which cells are taken.</p><p >Two parts can never claim the same cell.</p><p >Multiple stages, per-mode visibility, and target following on each axis.</p><p >Distance fading and line softness are adjustable.</p><p >Drawn with a single quad and a procedural shader, so it costs almost nothing.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Grouping System</strong></p><p >Parts placed near each other form a structure on their own.</p><p >Groups merge and split as parts are placed, moved or destroyed.</p><p >Groups are what make batching and collapse possible.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Batching System</strong></p><p >Merges the meshes of a group, per material, to cut draw calls.</p><p >Nearby groups unbatch when you enter a build mode, and batch again when you leave.</p><p >Colliders are left alone, so physics and raycasts keep working.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Area</strong></p><p >A sphere or a box where building is checked against Building Rules.</p><p >Areas can overlap, and the highest priority wins.</p><p >A part counts as inside when it is partly, or fully, within the bounds.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Rules</strong></p><p >Rules restrict categories, capacity, height and proximity.</p><p >Allow or block categories, cap how many parts are placed,</p><p >limit build height, or keep parts apart.</p><p >Rules can also be set globally on the Building Manager, with no area involved.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Physics System</strong></p><p >A structure stands as long as it still reaches anchored support.</p><p >Support is tracked across the whole structure, part by part.</p><p >Remove a load-bearing part, and everything above it can lose support and fall.</p><p >An idle structure costs almost nothing to check.</p><p >Checks are spread over several frames, and the rate is yours to tune.</p><p >Support is drawn green or red in the Scene View, before you even press Play.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Save System</strong></p><p >Saves every placed part: transform, socket, group, variant and state.</p><p >Store it in PlayerPrefs, in the project folder, or in the persistent data path.</p><p >Save on your own call, or automatically at a fixed interval.</p><p >Each scene gets its own slot, and a backup file sits next to the save.</p><p >Use the save and load callbacks to store your own data with a part.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Collections</strong></p><p >A named list of Building Part references, saved as an asset.</p><p >Reuse it in menus, in Building Input, or from your own code.</p><p >Pick one from a searchable field in the Inspector.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Menus UI</strong></p><p >A scrollable Catalog menu and a Radial menu, both on the same base class.</p><p >Categories hold slots, and each slot runs one action.</p><p >Standalone, mobile and gamepad input modes are supported.</p><p >Add your own slot actions when you need something else.</p></br><p ><strong>Networking</strong></p><p >Optional, and the core framework never depends on it.</p><p >One adapter replicates the building commands.</p><p >Another handles authority, and keeps save and load in sync.</p><p >Every building action already goes through the command system,</p><p >so the same setup serves single player and multiplayer.</p><p >FishNet, Mirror and PUN 2 are included, each with a player and a sample scene.</p></br><p ><strong>Sample Scenes</strong></p><p >Feature scenes for Socket, Grid, Area, Batching, Save and Collapse.</p><p >Ready-to-play examples for First Person, Third Person, Top Down, Terrain,</p><p >Mobile Touch, Gamepad, VR, Catalog Menu and Radial Menu.</p><p >The VR sample needs the XR Interaction Toolkit and the new Input System.</p><p >Every other sample runs in a default supported project.</p></br><p ><strong>Extensions</strong></p><p >Four project templates, each a different game style.</p><p >Buggy Constructor: grab parts with the mouse and snap a vehicle together.</p><p >Interior Builder: place furniture freely inside a pre-built house.</p><p >Survival Blueprint: carry resources to a ghost blueprint until it is built.</p><p >Survival Upgradable: upgrade placed parts through material tiers.</p></br><p ><strong>Integrations</strong></p><p >Game Creator 2: visual scripting instructions for building control.</p><p >Game Creator 2 Inventory: inventory-based building with resource costs.</p><p >PlayMaker: FSM actions for building control.</p><p >PUN 2, Mirror and FishNet: multiplayer building.</p>","compatibilityInfo":"","customLicense":false,"rating":{"average":5,"count":193,"__typename":"PackageRating"},"currentVersion":{"id":"1490096","name":"7.1.4","publishedDate":"2026-09-23T02:38:27Z","__typename":"ProductVersion"},"reviewCount":150,"downloadSize":"120674768","assetCount":1104,"publisher":{"id":"15834","name":"Mind From an open world game to a simple casual mobile game, it comes handy when a car needs nicer engine sound!</p></br><p >The asset is uploaded with Unity 6, but it can also work with older versions.</p></br><p >Every engine sound can be previewed in an <strong>interactive demo scene</strong> featuring realistic vehicle behavior simulation. Test dynamic engine RPM and load response, transmission behavior, switch between interior and exterior camera views, and rotate the camera around the vehicle to hear how the engine sound changes from different camera positions. Thanks to the included intake and exhaust sounds, the vehicle sounds noticeably different when listening from the front or rear of the vehicle.</p></br><p ><strong>50 complete engine sound packs</strong> - This depending on the prefab's optimization settings and it can be disabled too. By default it is set to \"Pause\" mode.</li><li >Change the engine sound based on how the camera looks at the vehicle. <strong>It will sound differently</strong> if the camera is looking at the front or at the back of the car, thanks to the i<strong>ntake audio clips</strong> and the <strong>Dynamic Audio Mixer.</strong></li><li >Enhance engine sounds with an <strong>\"aggressiveness\" SFX, </strong>suitable for simulating engine upgrades in a street racing game, mimicking open exhaust systems, a lost muffler, etc. :D</li><li >Add <strong>valve SFX, </strong>which will play valve sounds synchronized with the vehicle's engine RPM without altering the sound clip's pitch. Ideal for simulating engine noise in the vehicle's engine bay, it's also useful for mimicing that when the vehicle's hood got opened (when player does an engine repair or something like that). For example: enable this SFX when the player opened the engine hood.</li><li ><strong>Engine load \"wobble\"</strong> will \"wobble\" the engine load value for a short time right after the player hit the gas pedal. Used to mimic racecar, or truck engine behaviour.</li><li ><strong>Wind noise SFX</strong> adds a subtle yet crucial detail that might go unnoticed if it's there, but you'll definitely notice its absence. It adds a sense of realism and completeness to the experience.</li><li ><strong>Reverse gear SFX </strong>plays a whining noise when the vehicle is going in reverse.</li><li ><strong>Reverse gear's beeping warning sound</strong> is played when the vehicle is in reverse gear. By default, this SFX is disabled, but you can enable it for any engine sound prefab.</li></ul></br><p >Realistic Engine Sounds 2 is available in three editions: <strong>Lite</strong>, <strong>Plus</strong>, and <strong>Pro</strong>.</p><p >You can browse all editions <a href=\"https://assetstore.unity.com/lists/list-185177\" target=\"_blank\"><strong>here</strong></a>. complete modular building framework for Unity. Everything you need to implement a full building system in any project. Extensible, source-included, and built to scale.","keyFeatures":"<p ><strong>Building Manager</strong></p><p >One Building Manager per scene, and everything registers to it.</p><p >Building Parts, Sockets, Areas and Groups sign in when they turn on.</p><p >Look them up by type, by ID, or by distance.</p><p >It also owns the sub-systems: Grid, Grouping, Batching, Save, Terrain and Physics.</p><p >Turn on only the ones your game needs.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Controller</strong></p><p >Goes on your player or your camera.</p><p >Holds the current build mode, the active view and the selected Building Part.</p><p >Sends the player input to whichever Building State is active.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Input</strong></p><p >Reads validate, cancel, rotate and select, then hands them to the active state.</p><p >Works with the legacy Input Manager and the new Input System, out of the box.</p><p >Keyboard, mouse, gamepad and touch are all covered.</p><p >Building input stops while the cursor is over your UI.</p></br><p ><strong>Building States</strong></p><p >One state per build mode, each with its own Enter, Update and Exit.</p><p >Placement previews the part, snaps to sockets, rotates and aligns to the grid.</p><p >Destruction removes a placed part, with its own feedback.</p><p >Adjustment moves a placed part while keeping its connections.</p><p >Upgrade switches a placed part to another variant, in place.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingState to add your own mode.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Views</strong></p><p >A view answers one question: where does the player aim right now?</p><p >First Person, Third Person, Top Down and Orbital are included.</p><p >Each one builds its own build ray, and you can swap views while the game runs.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingView to support your own camera.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Part</strong></p><p >The placeable object, set up entirely in the Inspector.</p><p >Preview visuals, placement, conditions and behaviors all live on one component.</p><p >Presets let several parts share the same setup.</p><p >Renderer variants cover visual styles and upgrade tiers.</p><p >An editor tool turns any model into a ready-to-use prefab, in one click.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Conditions</strong></p><p >A condition decides whether an action is allowed, and says why when it is not.</p><p >They check socket attachment, areas, collisions, ground contact, support,</p><p >terrain layers, nearby trees and slope limits.</p><p >Conditions run in order, and the first failure blocks the action.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingCondition to write your own.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Behaviors</strong></p><p >A behavior reacts once the action has already happened, so it never blocks.</p><p >Play an animation on placement, destruction, adjustment or upgrade.</p><p >Spawn physics debris when a part is destroyed or collapses.</p><p >Inherit from BuildingBehavior for your own effects.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Socket</strong></p><p >A snap point where Building Parts attach to each other.</p><p >One socket holds several snapping points, each with its own offset.</p><p >Filter what may connect, by Building Part or by category.</p><p >Priorities decide which socket wins when two of them are close.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Grid System</strong></p><p >Snaps previews to fixed cells, and tracks which cells are taken.</p><p >Two parts can never claim the same cell.</p><p >Multiple stages, per-mode visibility, and target following on each axis.</p><p >Distance fading and line softness are adjustable.</p><p >Drawn with a single quad and a procedural shader, so it costs almost nothing.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Grouping System</strong></p><p >Parts placed near each other form a structure on their own.</p><p >Groups merge and split as parts are placed, moved or destroyed.</p><p >Groups are what make batching and collapse possible.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Batching System</strong></p><p >Merges the meshes of a group, per material, to cut draw calls.</p><p >Nearby groups unbatch when you enter a build mode, and batch again when you leave.</p><p >Colliders are left alone, so physics and raycasts keep working.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Area</strong></p><p >A sphere or a box where building is checked against Building Rules.</p><p >Areas can overlap, and the highest priority wins.</p><p >A part counts as inside when it is partly, or fully, within the bounds.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Rules</strong></p><p >Rules restrict categories, capacity, height and proximity.</p><p >Allow or block categories, cap how many parts are placed,</p><p >limit build height, or keep parts apart.</p><p >Rules can also be set globally on the Building Manager, with no area involved.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Physics System</strong></p><p >A structure stands as long as it still reaches anchored support.</p><p >Support is tracked across the whole structure, part by part.</p><p >Remove a load-bearing part, and everything above it can lose support and fall.</p><p >An idle structure costs almost nothing to check.</p><p >Checks are spread over several frames, and the rate is yours to tune.</p><p >Support is drawn green or red in the Scene View, before you even press Play.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Save System</strong></p><p >Saves every placed part: transform, socket, group, variant and state.</p><p >Store it in PlayerPrefs, in the project folder, or in the persistent data path.</p><p >Save on your own call, or automatically at a fixed interval.</p><p >Each scene gets its own slot, and a backup file sits next to the save.</p><p >Use the save and load callbacks to store your own data with a part.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Collections</strong></p><p >A named list of Building Part references, saved as an asset.</p><p >Reuse it in menus, in Building Input, or from your own code.</p><p >Pick one from a searchable field in the Inspector.</p></br><p ><strong>Building Menus UI</strong></p><p >A scrollable Catalog menu and a Radial menu, both on the same base class.</p><p >Categories hold slots, and each slot runs one action.</p><p >Standalone, mobile and gamepad input modes are supported.</p><p >Add your own slot actions when you need something else.</p></br><p ><strong>Networking</strong></p><p >Optional, and the core framework never depends on it.</p><p >One adapter replicates the building commands.</p><p >Another handles authority, and keeps save and load in sync.</p><p >Every building action already goes through the command system,</p><p >so the same setup serves single player and multiplayer.</p><p >FishNet, Mirror and PUN 2 are included, each with a player and a sample scene.</p></br><p ><strong>Sample Scenes</strong></p><p >Feature scenes for Socket, Grid, Area, Batching, Save and Collapse.</p><p >Ready-to-play examples for First Person, Third Person, Top Down, Terrain,</p><p >Mobile Touch, Gamepad, VR, Catalog Menu and Radial Menu.</p><p >The VR sample needs the XR Interaction Toolkit and the new Input System.</p><p >Every other sample runs in a default supported project.</p></br><p ><strong>Extensions</strong></p><p >Four project templates, each a different game style.</p><p >Buggy Constructor: grab parts with the mouse and snap a vehicle together.</p><p through material tiers.</p></br><p ><strong>Integrations</strong></p><p >Game Creator 2: visual scripting instructions for building control.</p><p >Game Creator 2 Inventory: inventory-based building with resource costs.</p><p >PlayMaker: FSM actions for building control.</p><p >PUN 2, Mirror and FishNet: multiplayer This depending on the prefab's optimization settings and it can be disabled too. By default it is set to \"Pause\" mode.</li><li >Change the engine sound based on how the camera looks at the vehicle. <strong>It will sound differently</strong> if the camera is looking at the front or at the back of the car, thanks to the i<strong>ntake audio clips</strong> and the <strong>Dynamic Audio Mixer.</strong></li><li >Enhance engine sounds with an <strong>\"aggressiveness\" SFX, </strong>suitable for simulating engine upgrades in a street racing game, mimicking open exhaust systems, a lost muffler, etc. :D</li><li >Add <strong>valve SFX, </strong>which will play valve sounds synchronized with the vehicle's engine RPM without altering the sound clip's pitch. Ideal for simulating engine noise in the vehicle's engine bay, it's also useful for mimicing that when the vehicle's hood got opened (when player does an engine repair or something like that). For example: enable this SFX when the player opened the engine hood.</li><li ><strong>Engine load \"wobble\"</strong> will \"wobble\" the engine load value for a short time right after the player hit the gas pedal. Used to mimic racecar, or truck engine behaviour.</li><li ><strong>Wind noise SFX</strong> adds a subtle yet crucial detail that might go unnoticed if it's there, but you'll definitely notice its absence. It adds a sense of realism and completeness to the experience.</li><li ><strong>Reverse gear SFX </strong>plays a whining noise when the vehicle is going in reverse.</li><li ><strong>Reverse gear's beeping warning sound</strong> is played when the vehicle is in reverse gear. By default, this SFX is disabled, but you can enable it for any engine sound prefab.</li></ul></br><p >Realistic Engine Sounds 2 is available in three editions: <strong>Lite</strong>, <strong>Plus</strong>, and <strong>Pro</strong>.</p><p >You can browse all editions <a href=\"https://assetstore.unity.com/lists/list-185177\" target=\"_blank\"><strong>here</strong></a>.
